Keeps all crew comms inside the manager app instead of a personal group chat, which is both more professional to show the owner and gives Luna visibility into team comms.)
- Availability + time-off requests as structured data (not texts): Capturing who's available/unavailable and time-off requests as approvable records (vs. remembering a text) is the unglamorous backbone that makes everything else — scheduling, swaps, coverage — possible, and prevents the double-booking that wrecks a Saturday. (fit: Right-sized version: a simple time_off / availability request flow staff submit and Bradley approves, feeding the Calendar's existing conflict logic. High utility, low complexity, and it makes Luna smarter about coverage warnings ('Two bartenders requested off Sat — you have an event booked'). Build the data model even before a full schedule UI; it's the cheap foundation.)

### Tripleseat / Perfect Venue / HoneyBook — private-event sales category (leads -> BEO/proposal -> e-sign -> deposit -> event CRM). Tripleseat = enterprise standard (chains/hotels, deep but heavy, "challenging on phone," pricey, features go untouched at single venues). Perfect Venue = the right-sized, mobile-first, restaurant-native one (lead inbox, autofill templates, deposits, $119-199/mo). HoneyBook = the UX gold standard for the document flow: ONE "smart file" that is proposal + contract + invoice combined — client reviews, e-signs, and pays in a single uninterrupted step, with view/sign/pay tracking and conditional follow-up automations. For a single seaside bar run from a phone, steal Perfect Venue's shape, HoneyBook's flow, and only the lightest of Tripleseat's BEO/portal ideas.
- All-in-one 'one link' proposal: view + e-sign + pay deposit in a single flow (HoneyBook smart file / Tripleseat guest portal): HoneyBook's core insight: don't send three files (proposal, contract, invoice) and chase signatures over email — send ONE link where the client reviews the package, e-signs, and pays the deposit in one uninterrupted step. Tripleseat calls its version the 'silent salesperson' / 'single source of truth' that closes deals faster because there's zero friction and zero back-and-forth. Eliminating the gap between 'yes' and 'paid' is the single biggest conversion lever in this entire category. (fit: This is THE highest-value steal and it sits directly on top of what's already built. Iggy's already has parties, party_packages, and InvoicePanel/invoice.ts generating the numbers. Add a public, tokenized, no-login route (e.g. /p/:partyToken served from the public site, reading the party + line items via an edge function with RLS-safe access) that renders the proposal, an e-sign checkbox/typed-name + timestamp + IP stored on the party row (status inquiry->confirmed), and a Stripe deposit button. This finally wires the already-installed-but-stubbed Stripe checkout — write a create-deposit-session edge function (mirrors the existing send-party-email/availability pattern) and a stripe-webhook fn that flips confirmation tracking + deposit_paid on the party. One link from Bradley's phone behind the bar = signed contract + money in. Bring the same checkout to the Shop's stub alert as a bonus.)
- Lead inbox: every inquiry auto-lands as a structured lead in one dashboard, with status pipeline: Perfect Venue's central promise is 'all incoming inquiries automatically flow into your dashboard' so nothing lives lost in an email account, and the team tracks proposal status + communication history in one place. The pipeline (lead/inquiry -> proposal sent -> confirmed -> deposit paid) is what turns a pile of messages into a sales funnel you can actually work and forecast from. (fit: Already 90% there and just needs unifying. The BookEvent form already hits submit-booking-request, Contact hits submit-contact-message, and there's a Messages/Inbox with Gmail sync. The steal is making the booking-request edge function CREATE a party row (status='inquiry') and a contact, not just a message — so every web inquiry becomes a real lead in the Parties pipeline automatically, with the email thread attached. Then add a simple Kanban/segmented view of parties by status on the Parties page (inquiry | proposal sent | confirmed | done). Bradley sees his whole event funnel at a glance on his phone. This also feeds the follow_up_date that already exists on the party record.)
- Auto-filling email + document templates ('send proposals 3x faster'): Perfect Venue's templates auto-merge event info (name, date, guest count, space, package, totals) so a manager replies and sends a polished proposal in seconds instead of retyping. Tripleseat's BEO templates do the same for the kitchen/floor doc. Speed-to-respond is the #1 booking predictor; merge fields are how you get it without sacrificing polish. (fit: message_templates and party emails already exist — extend templates with merge tokens ({{guest_count}}, {{space}}, {{event_date}}, {{package_total}}, {{deposit_amount}}, {{portal_link}}) resolved server-side in send-party-email. This is a small, high-leverage add. Crucially, this is ALSO the seam for Luna: instead of static templates, Luna (via the bridge, writing a draft row Bradley approves) can compose a context-aware proposal email referencing the customer's last_event_date or tags — turning 'mail merge' into 'AI sales assistant' with no extra UI. Keep a one-tap template fallback for when Luna isn't needed.)
- Lightweight BEO / run-of-show as an internal, shareable event sheet: The Banquet Event Order is the operational backbone — one sheet with timeline, headcount, menu, space, special requests, allergies, point of contact — that the kitchen and floor work off. Tripleseat lets you build a branded BEO in a few clicks and clients can view/comment on it. It's the artifact that prevents day-of chaos. (fit: Iggy's has all the BEO data on the party record (guest_count, space, food/drink service type, hours, totals) plus packages/line items — it just doesn't render the operational view. Add a clean, printable/phone-readable BEO view per party (internal-only by default, optionally exposed read-only in the guest portal) that the bartender/kitchen can pull up day-of. Keep it ONE screen. Do NOT replicate Tripleseat's multi-page configurable BEO builder — that's exactly the bloat a single bar never uses.)
- Automated follow-up cadence: deposit reminder, balance-due, day-before confirm, post-event rebook: Perfect Venue 'puts reminder emails on autopilot' — payment reminders, event reminders, status updates triggered by real activity. HoneyBook's conditional automations ('only send follow-up if client hasn't signed within 3 days') are the retention/cash-flow engine: deals don't die from being forgotten, deposits get collected, and post-event 'come back / refer a friend' notes drive repeat bookings. (fit: This is the second marquee steal and it's where the Luna bridge becomes a genuine COMPETITIVE ADVANTAGE rather than a me-too feature. Don't hardcode a brittle rule engine. Home-lab Luna already polls tables and pushes luna_insights on a schedule — give it read access to parties (follow_up_date, status, deposit_paid, event_date) and let it (a) push an alert insight when a proposal's been unsigned for N days or a deposit is overdue, and (b) draft the follow-up email as a pending row Bradley one-taps to send. Same for day-before confirmations and post-event thank-you/rebook using contacts.marketing_opt_in + last_event_date. Competitors send a canned reminder; Luna reasons about the specific deal. This is the demo moment for the owner.)
- View/sign/pay activity tracking ('the client opened it'): HoneyBook lets you 'track if clients have viewed, signed, or paid so nothing slips through the cracks.' Knowing a proposal was opened (or NOT opened) tells the manager exactly who to nudge and when — it makes follow-up surgical instead of guesswork, and it's a quiet trust signal that the business is on top of things. (fit: Cheap to add and it supercharges the automation steal. Stamp the party record with proposal_viewed_at (the portal route pings an edge function on load), signed_at, deposit_paid_at. Surface these as small status chips on the Parties pipeline card. Feed the same timestamps to Luna so 'viewed 4 days ago, never signed' becomes a precise, well-timed nudge. No heavyweight analytics dashboard — just the three timestamps that actually change behavior.)
- Online, bookable group packages (catalog -> client selects -> auto-builds proposal): HoneyBook's brochure/proposal flow lets clients SELECT services and auto-drops them into a proposal; Perfect Venue lets guests view availability, pick menus, and submit deposits online. Surfacing packages publicly does pre-qualification and price-anchoring for you, and a self-serve 'request this package' shortens the path to a booked event. (fit: Directly answers Open Ask #1 (group packages listed/bookable online). The packages and party_packages tables already exist with flat/per_person/per_hour pricing. Render them as public package cards on the site (or within BookEvent), and let a selection pre-populate a party + party_packages line items on submit — so the inquiry arrives already half-quoted. Keep it to a few curated packages (upstairs / downstairs / whole-venue tiers), NOT an exhaustive a-la-carte configurator. Pre-fill, then let Bradley/Luna finalize.)
- Unified events-and-parties calendar with space conflict / double-booking prevention: Every platform leads with 'see all your events at a glance and avoid double bookings.' For a venue with shared spaces, the calendar IS the product — a confirmed party and a public DJ night can't both own 'upstairs' at the same time, and catching that conflict before it happens is the whole job. (fit: Already built (Calendar route unifies events + parties with availability/conflict logic, spaces upstairs/downstairs/whole). The steal is hardening + finishing it against the public side: make BookEvent's availability edge function authoritative against BOTH events and confirmed parties so the public can never request an already-taken space/time, and address Open Ask #2 with real two-way Google Calendar sync (the google-calendar edge fn + party/event sync already exist — extend to subscribe/pull external busy times). This is reinforcement of an existing strength, not new surface area.)

### Popmenu / BentoBox / Sociavore (restaurant website + online ordering + interactive menus + events/catering + automated marketing). Cross-teardown for a single seaside bar run from a phone, mapped onto Iggy's existing React/Vite/TS + Supabase + Stripe(stub) + Google APIs + home-lab Luna bridge stack.
- Interactive menu items: per-dish 'love it' reactions, photos, reviews, and a 'remind me' / follow action: Popmenu's core mechanic. A static menu is a dead end; an interactive one captures intent and identity. Guests tap a heart on a dish, leave a one-line review, or ask to be reminded about an item — every tap is a first-party data point and a hook back. It also surfaces fan-favorites socially ('most-loved cocktail'), which is its own merchandising. (fit: You already have DB-driven menus (appetizers, on_tap, off_tap, cocktails, shots) and Supabase Realtime. Add a lightweight `menu_reactions` table (item_ref, type love/review/remind, guest_id nullable, text). Public site renders a heart + count on each cocktail/beer card; manager Dashboard gets a 'most-loved this week' insight. No guest login required for a love (anonymous + localStorage), email only for 'remind me'. This is a few-day build on infra you own, and it directly feeds Luna: 'Sailor's Mojito got 40 loves and 0 inventory — push it as the weekend special.')
- Per-dish SEO landing pages (one indexable URL per menu item, fed by reviews/likes): Popmenu's quietly-genius SEO play: every dish gets its own crawlable page with description, photo, and guest activity, so 'best mojito near me' and 'fish tacos [town]' land on the bar's site instead of Yelp. For a seaside/tourist bar, 'near me' + dish-level search is exactly how walk-ins find you. It's free organic traffic that compounds with the reaction data above. (fit: Your menu is already in Postgres and the site is React+Vite on Netlify. Add static/SSG-style routes like /menu/cocktails/sailors-mojito with proper title/meta/JSON-LD (schema.org Menu/MenuItem) generated from the DB at build or via Netlify edge. Inject the love-count and any review snippet so Google sees fresh, unique content per page. Near-zero ongoing cost, and it's the single highest-ROI thing a seaside tourist bar can do that none of these platforms do better than a hand-rolled site.)
- Guest identity + lightweight CRM (capture email/phone at the point of interaction, segment by behavior): This is the actual engine under all three platforms — Popmenu explicitly markets 'own the relationship with your guests' and identifies new vs returning. The website's job is to convert anonymous traffic into a contactable, segmentable list (favorite dishes, last visit, birthday, opt-in). Everything downstream — automated marketing, win-back, event invites — depends on this list existing. (fit: You ALREADY have a `contacts` table with tags, marketing_opt_in, and last_event_date — but it's currently fed only by the Parties module. Extend it to be the single guest spine: capture from the menu 'remind me', the Shop checkout, the BookEvent form, and the contact form, all writing to `contacts` with a source tag. Supabase Auth is installed, so optional guest accounts are cheap. This is the unlock that makes the marketing-automation feature below possible — build it first.)
- Events-to-deposit pipeline: bookable packages online -> auto-proposal -> deposit captured via Stripe: BentoBox's events module is the most enterprise-validated workflow here: website inquiry -> smart form routes details -> proposal/BEO with contract terms -> partial or full deposit collected online. It compresses the back-and-forth and locks money in early. This maps almost 1:1 onto what Iggy's owner explicitly asked for (open asks #1 group packages bookable online, #3 Stripe deposits). (fit: You're 80% there and don't realize it. You have `packages` (flat/per_person/per_hour), `parties`, `party_packages`, `InvoicePanel`/invoice.ts, send-party-email, and Google Calendar sync. What's missing is (a) surfacing the packages catalog on the PUBLIC BookEvent page so guests pick a package + space + date against your existing availability logic, and (b) wiring real Stripe checkout to collect a deposit that creates/updates a `parties` row in status 'inquiry'->'confirmed'. Steal BentoBox's exact funnel shape (inquiry -> proposal -> deposit -> calendar hold) but run it through your invoice + Google Calendar plumbing. This is the headline demo for the owner.)
- Commission-free online ordering / pickup tab (direct, not third-party): All three hammer 'commission-free, no third-party fees' — it's the #1 reason restaurants leave DoorDash/Toast-style middlemen. For a bar with food categories already structured (menu_categories, menu_items, menu_item_options), a simple order-ahead / 'start a tab' / pickup flow keeps 100% of the ticket and, critically, captures the guest into your CRM (vs the marketplace owning that data). (fit: Scope this CAREFULLY — a full KDS/POS is the bloat trap (see antipatterns). For a seaside bar the right size is order-ahead pickup or a pre-order for events, reusing menu_items + menu_item_options for modifiers and Stripe for payment, writing to your existing `orders` table. Don't build delivery, driver dispatch, or table-side fire-to-kitchen. The win is the merch Shop checkout (open ask #3) + simple food pre-order, both on the same Stripe integration you'll build for deposits.)
- Automated marketing autopilot: behavior-triggered email/SMS/social tied to dishes, birthdays, last-visit: Popmenu's flagship paid feature — automated, personalized emails/texts based on favorite dishes, reviews, birthdays, and a monthly calendar of brand social posts 'with zero manual effort.' Restaurants pay $300+/mo for this. It works because it fires at the right moment from data the restaurant already has, with no human in the loop. (fit: This is where Iggy's can LEAP the competition because you have Luna. You don't build a campaign-builder UI — you give the home-lab Luna bridge read access to contacts + menu_reactions + events + specials + inventory, plus proactive INSIGHT cadences, and let her DRAFT the sends by writing rows back (a `campaigns`/`scheduled_sends` table the app renders for one-tap approval via Gmail edge fn + existing Specials studio for the visual). Luna becomes the marketing autopilot: 'Friday DJ Night + 30 contacts who loved the mojito + sunset tide chart -> here's a drafted email and an Instagram-ready Special, approve to send.' Frame to the owner: Popmenu sells this as AI marketing for hundreds a month; Iggy's has a smarter version because Luna actually knows the bar's inventory, events, and tide. Honor open ask #4 by pairing it with a real social auto-post path (Instagram Graph API via an edge fn) so it's not just the manual share sheet.)
- Ticketed / prepaid experiences (sell tickets to DJ Night, trivia, wine tasting, themed nights up front): BentoBox's ticketed-events module: sell tickets for workshops, tastings, themed nights with zero fees, track sales in the dashboard. It turns your events calendar from a free listing into a revenue line and guarantees attendance with prepayment — perfect for capacity-limited upstairs/downstairs nights. (fit: You already have a rich `events` table (DJ Night, Live Music, Karaoke, Trivia, Themed, Holiday) with spaces and time windows, plus a public Events calendar. Add an optional 'ticketed' flag + price + capacity to events, a Stripe checkout (same integration), and a simple `event_tickets` table. The public Events page gets a 'Get Tickets' button on flagged events. Low effort on top of infra you have, and it's a second compelling owner demo: 'our themed nights now pre-sell.')
- Gift cards + branded merch storefront on the same checkout: BentoBox: sell gift cards and branded merch directly, no third-party fees, redeemable on-site. Gift cards are pure float + new-customer acquisition (the buyer gifts you a stranger), and a seaside bar with a brand has real merch pull (tees, hats, koozies for tourists). (fit: Your Shop page already has merch + a cart with a STUB Stripe alert — finishing that one Stripe checkout function (open ask #3) instantly unlocks merch sales. Add gift cards as a product type that issues a redeemable code (a `gift_cards` table) — modest extra work once Stripe is live, and a tourist-friendly revenue stream. One checkout integration powers Shop + deposits + tickets + gift cards; build it once.)
- Real-time menu availability / 86-ing tied to inventory (time-based and stock-based menu states): Sociavore highlights conditional availability — time-based menus (lunch vs dinner, happy hour) and on-the-fly 86-ing to reflect stock. Showing a sold-out or off-hours item erodes trust; auto-hiding it is invisible polish that prevents the worst guest moment (ordering something unavailable). (fit: You have happy_hour as its own category and an Inventory system with low-stock logic and par levels. Tie the two: when an inventory_item linked to a menu item drops to zero/low, auto-flag the menu item as '86'd' or low on the public site (greyed with a subtle 'ask your server'), and surface happy-hour items only during the window using your minutes-from-midnight model. Luna can even proactively suggest the 86 from the order-scanner/usage logs. This is a uniquely Iggy's capability — none of these platforms connect a real inventory system to the public menu.)
- Single operator dashboard with revenue + engagement at a glance (the 'one screen behind the bar' view): BentoBox's restaurant dashboard centralizes sales from orders, tickets, and gift cards; Popmenu's owners app surfaces guest activity. The value for a solo phone-run operation is a single glanceable screen: today's bookings, ticket sales, low stock, new loves, unread messages — no app-hopping. (fit: You already have a Dashboard with a Luna card + insights feed — this is the right home for it; just make it the revenue+engagement cockpit. Aggregate: today's parties (you have the widget), today's events/ticket sales, low-stock (have it), unread messages (have it), and the new 'most-loved this week.' Keep it ONE screen optimized for a phone behind the bar. Luna's insights feed is the differentiator — it's not just metrics, it's 'here's what to do about them.')

### Marqii + Untappd for Business (digital hospitality / listings + menu sync, and the beer-community check-in loop)
- Single source of truth -> publish-everywhere sync (Marqii's core): Marqii's entire $75-150/mo value prop is that you edit a menu/hour/price ONCE and it propagates to Google, Yelp, Apple Maps, Facebook, and your website automatically and near-instantly. The pain it kills is real: stale, contradictory menus and hours across the web that lose walk-in traffic. For a single bar, the highest-leverage targets are Google Business Profile (the listing 90% of seaside foot traffic actually checks) and the public site (already DB-driven, so it's free). (fit: Iggy's already has the source of truth -- MenuManager writes to on_tap/off_tap/cocktails/etc and the public site reads it live. That's half of Marqii built. STEAL the outbound leg: a 'Publish' action on MenuManager/Events that, beyond updating the site, pushes to Google Business Profile (hours, menu, a 'what's on tap today' post) via a new edge function, mirroring the existing google-calendar/gmail pattern. Scope it to ONE listing (Google) not 80 -- that single integration captures most of the real-world value for a seaside bar without enterprise plumbing. Luna can own the cadence: 'tap list changed at 4pm, pushed updated beer menu to Google + posted to the site.')
- Embeddable auto-updating menu + hours widgets: Marqii sells brand-matched menu/hours widgets you embed on your site that update automatically when the dashboard changes, so the public site never drifts from reality. It removes the 'who forgot to update the website' failure mode entirely. (fit: Iggy's public site is already DB-driven so this is mostly DONE -- but make it EXPLICIT and bulletproof: a single live 'Hours / Today's Status' component (open/closed now, happy hour live, today's events) sourced from the same tables, plus an '86'd / sold out' flag on menu items so the public Beers/Cocktails pages instantly reflect a keg blowing. Cheap to add, directly mirrors what Marqii charges for, and the 86 toggle is something Bradley can hit from his phone behind the bar mid-shift.)
- AI Suggested Review Response + reputation inbox (Marqii): Marqii's newest flagship: AI drafts on-brand, SEO-friendly replies to Google/Yelp reviews (cut response time from 90s to under 30s, 70% time savings), with tone settings per positive/neutral/negative review, plus sentiment + keyword analytics to spot trends. Review velocity and owner responses materially move Google ranking and walk-in conversion. This is pure owner-meeting gold and Iggy's has zero reputation surface today. (fit: This is the single best Luna fit in the whole teardown. Build a 'Reputation' surface that pulls recent Google reviews (Places/Business Profile API via edge function) into a table, and let Luna DRAFT replies as luna_insights/pending rows -- exactly the Messages/Inbox pattern Iggy's already runs for Gmail. Bradley approves/edits/sends from his phone; Luna handles tone (warm seaside-bar voice) and flags the angry ones as alerts. Luna's memory substrate means replies can reference real context ('glad you caught Trivia Night'). Owner sees: 'we now answer every review within the hour, in our voice, automatically drafted.')
- The check-in / on-premise customer loop (Untappd's moat): Untappd's stickiest mechanic isn't the menu -- it's that customers physically in your bar generate live signal: who's here now, what they're drinking, ratings, and you can 'toast' them back (they get a push notification). It turns the menu into a two-way community channel and gives the bar real demographic + popularity data (trending styles, top beers, unique users within a radius, menu views by source). It's marketing reach you don't pay for. (fit: Don't integrate Untappd itself (enterprise, beer-only, off-platform). STEAL the IDEA at Iggy's scale: a lightweight 'on the floor right now' signal. Cheapest version -- a guest-facing QR at the table/bar that opens the live menu AND lets a guest tap a beer/cocktail as a soft 'check-in' or rate it; that feeds a Supabase table Luna reads to tell Bradley 'the Hazy IPA is moving fast tonight, the Margarita is dragging.' It marries to Iggy's existing Inventory (usage signal) and gives Luna real demand data to suggest specials. Even without guest-facing UI, Luna can synthesize this from inventory_logs usage velocity -- a 'what's hot tonight' insight card -- delivering Untappd's analytics value with data Iggy's ALREADY captures.)
- Beer-database autofill for menu building (Untappd): Untappd auto-populates a new menu entry from a 3M-beer database -- type a beer name and get ABV, style, brewery, description, even artwork. It removes the tedious data entry that makes operators avoid keeping the menu current; the menu stays accurate because adding an item is 5 seconds, not 5 minutes. (fit: When Bradley adds a beer to on_tap/off_tap in MenuManager, let Luna (or a small edge function hitting a beer data source / web lookup) autofill ABV, style, brewery, and a tasting-note description from just the name. This directly attacks the reason bar menus go stale -- friction -- and plays to Luna's strengths (she can draft the description in Iggy's voice). Same trick already half-exists in the Order Scanner (invoice -> line items -> match inventory); extend that enrichment to the public menu.)
- TV / digital-signage menu mode: Both platforms push the same menu to a TV display (Untappd via an Amazon Signage Stick; Marqii via display output) so the in-venue screen is always live with no separate design work. For a bar, a glanceable behind-the-bar tap-list TV is genuinely useful and looks premium. (fit: Add a read-only, large-type '/tv' route on the PUBLIC site that renders the live tap list + tonight's events + happy-hour status from the same tables, auto-refreshing. Cast a cheap Fire Stick / old iPad to a screen behind the bar. Near-zero build (it's a styled view of data you already serve), high visual impact in the owner demo, and it makes the 'one source of truth' story tangible -- change a keg in MenuManager, the bar TV updates itself.)
- AI-assisted menu/promo design (Untappd's AI design tool + Marqii templates): Untappd recently shipped AI-powered digital menu design (auto-generate an on-brand menu layout); Marqii offers brand-matched templates and managed design services. The point is to lower the skill floor so good-looking menus/specials happen without a designer. (fit: Iggy's Specials canvas studio ALREADY beats this (layers, gradients, filters, GIF export, templates) -- so don't rebuild it. Instead steal the AI ASSIST on top: let Luna generate a first-draft special (copy + suggested layout/template choice) from a prompt like 'Friday DJ night, $5 well drinks,' landing as an editable draft in the Specials studio. Bradley polishes and shares. This is the 'AI design' headline both competitors now tout, delivered through infrastructure Iggy's already has, with Luna as the generator.)
- Multi-channel social publishing from one composer: Marqii lets you create and schedule social campaigns across channels from the same dashboard; Untappd publishes menus straight to Facebook and the Untappd app. The value is reach without context-switching, and scheduling so posts go out at peak times. (fit: This maps directly to the owner's KNOWN OPEN ASK for true social auto-posting (currently the Specials 'share to Instagram' is a manual share sheet). Steal the SCHEDULER + AUTO-PUBLISH: an edge function for Instagram/Facebook Graph API publishing, with Luna proposing a posting calendar ('post tomorrow's Trivia Night special at 11am Saturday') as approvable insight cards. Keep a human-approval gate -- the antipattern to avoid is fully autonomous posting -- but move from 'manual share' to 'Luna drafts + schedules, Bradley one-taps approve.')

### Resy / OpenTable / SevenRooms (reservation + waitlist + guest CRM + marketing-automation category)
- Unified Guest Profile (the 360 view) — promote contacts from party-only to bar-wide: SevenRooms' whole moat is one auto-built profile per guest carrying 100+ data points: visit count, lifetime/itemized spend, preferences, occasions (birthday/anniversary), notes, and tags. OpenTable splits it cleanly into 'visit notes' (this booking only) vs 'guestbook notes' (shows EVERY visit, e.g. 'Gluten Free', 'VIP'). That single record is what powers both better service and all downstream marketing — nothing fires without it. (fit: Iggy's contacts table already exists but is scoped to private-party clients. Generalize it into the bar's guestbook: add visit_count, total_spend (party + future merch/deposit Stripe), occasions (birthday/anniversary dates), guestbook_notes (persistent, e.g. 'always books downstairs', 'pours from the local IPA') vs visit_notes (per-event). Seed it from existing parties.contacts and messages senders. This is a small schema add, not a new product, and it instantly makes Luna's insights guest-aware ('Sarah's anniversary is in 9 days and she books a downstairs table every year').)
- Rules-based Auto-Tagging library: SevenRooms applies tags automatically from rules ('wine lover', 'positive reviewer', 'lapsed'), and OpenTable is now doing AI-assisted tags from booking/spend patterns. Tags are the join key between the profile and every campaign — segmentation is impossible without them, and manual tagging never happens behind a bar. (fit: This is a NATIVE Luna job, not a UI to build. Luna already reads every table on a cadence; give her a tag-rule pass: contact with 3+ confirmed parties -> 'regular'; last_event_date > 9 months -> 'lapsed'; marketing_opt_in + downstairs bookings -> 'private-event lead'; high gratuity_rate -> 'generous'. She writes the tags back to contacts. Zero new screens, and it turns the dormant tags column into a live segmentation engine.)
- Trigger-based marketing automation CATALOG (birthday / win-back / VIP / post-event): This is the feature competitors literally productize (SevenRooms '7X'): behavioral triggers fire timed email/SMS automatically — birthday & anniversary offers, lapsed-guest win-backs when visit frequency drops, VIP invites, post-visit follow-ups. It is the single highest-ROI thing on these platforms because it runs while the operator sleeps. (fit: Do NOT build a campaign-builder. Steal the trigger LIST and hand it to Luna as proactive insight cadences. Luna already pushes luna_insights and can draft party emails (send-party-email edge fn exists) and specials. Pattern: Luna detects the trigger from data, drafts the send (email via Gmail integration, or a specials canvas + share-sheet for social), and surfaces it as a 'suggestion' insight on Bradley's dashboard with an Approve button. Bradley taps approve behind the bar; the existing send-reply/send-party-email/Gmail rails do the delivery. This gives Iggy's the $500/mo SevenRooms feature for the cost of a few prompt cadences and an approval button — and it's honest with the owner: 'we automated the marketing without paying for a marketing platform.')
- Post-event / post-visit feedback survey with private-first review routing: SevenRooms sends an automated 1-tap star survey after the experience; a low rating routes to a private 'tell us more' page (catch it before it goes public), a high rating nudges the guest toward Google/Yelp. It builds the profile AND protects reputation AND harvests 5-star reviews — three wins from one automated email. (fit: Perfect fit for the private-event business specifically (where the email address is already captured and the stakes/spend are high). When a party flips to 'completed' (or follow_up_date hits), Luna drafts a one-question feedback email via the existing send-party-email rail. 4-5 stars -> link to the Google review; 1-3 stars -> link to Bradley/contact form so he saves it. Feedback writes back to the contact profile. Reuses Gmail + contacts + parties entirely; new surface is just a tiny public feedback landing page (the public site already has a Contact form pattern to clone).)
- Notify / waitlist — repurposed as Event RSVP + 'notify me' interest list: Resy's Notify isn't a host-stand queue — it's demand capture: guests raise their hand for a date/experience and get pinged when it opens, turning cancellations and future slots into bookings, and (critically) it ties back to the guest profile so you can prioritize good guests. It's the only reservation-platform mechanic that maps to a no-reservations seaside bar. (fit: Iggy's takes walk-ins, so skip table queuing. Instead: on the public Events calendar, add a 'Notify me / RSVP' button per event (DJ Night, Trivia, Themed). It captures email/phone into contacts with marketing_opt_in and an interest tag for that category. Two payoffs: (1) headcount signal for the bar, and (2) a pre-segmented list Luna can use — 'remind the 40 people who RSVP'd to karaoke that it's tonight.' Reuses the submit-booking-request edge-fn pattern; it's a button + a row insert, not a reservation engine.)
- Deposits & prepaid add-ons on bookings (the honest reason to finish Stripe): SevenRooms ties deposits/card-holds directly to high-demand bookings and private dining, showing minimum spends/room fees/deposits UPFRONT, and the deposit redeems against the final bill. It measurably cuts no-shows and is the cleanest revenue justification for taking payment online. (fit: This is the missing keystone for the already-built Parties + Invoices system and answers the owner's open ask #3 (real Stripe checkout) AND #1 (packages bookable online). When a party is confirmed, take a deposit via Stripe Checkout against the existing invoice; deposit shows on InvoicePanel and nets out of the final total. Same Stripe wiring then unblocks the merch Shop stub. Frame to the owner as: one Stripe integration unlocks event deposits (no-show protection) + online package booking + merch — three open asks closed by one build.)
- Occasion & preference data surfaced AT point-of-service ('service intelligence'): The under-appreciated half of these platforms: profile data isn't just for marketing, it's pushed to staff at the moment of service — OpenTable shows dietary/VIP/occasion notes on the reservation so the floor can deliver a personal touch. It's what makes a guest feel known and is the cheapest loyalty mechanic that exists. (fit: Iggy's runs from one phone, so the 'floor' is Bradley. On the dashboard / today's-parties widget, have Luna surface the relevant guestbook notes and occasions for tonight's confirmed parties ('Table downstairs at 8 — the Reillys, anniversary, she drinks the local IPA, tipped 25% last time'). Pure read of contacts + parties, rendered into the existing insights feed. Makes the boss see the data working in real time, not just in a campaign.)

### Later / Buffer / Sprout Social (social scheduling, content calendar, multi-platform auto-publish, analytics, link-in-bio)
- A real content QUEUE + posting schedule (Buffer's core model): Buffer's killer primitive is the posting schedule: you define preferred time slots once (e.g. 'Thu 4pm, Fri 5pm, Sun noon'), then 'Add to Queue' drops each post into the next open slot — no date-picking per post. Posting goals (1x/3x/5x per week) auto-populate the slots. It turns scheduling from a per-post decision into a fill-the-pipe habit, which is exactly how a busy operator sustains consistency. (fit: Iggy's content is inherently recurring and known in advance: weekly DJ Night, Trivia, Karaoke, Happy Hour, the daily/weekly Special. Add a scheduled_for timestamp + status (draft/queued/published) to the specials table and a tiny 'Iggy's posting schedule' (a few weekly slots). Bradley designs a Special in the existing canvas studio, hits 'Add to Queue', and it lands in the next bar-relevant slot instead of going out the instant he's slammed behind the bar. This is the single highest-leverage steal and it reuses SpecialEditor entirely.)
- True API auto-publish (closes Open Ask #4 — the manual share-sheet gap): Later's decisive advantage over Buffer is real auto-publish of Instagram feed posts, Reels AND Stories with zero manual intervention (for Business accounts via the Instagram Graph API / Content Publishing API). Buffer still falls back to 'Notify me' reminders for some formats — and that reminder model is precisely what Iggy's does today (navigator.share opens the phone share sheet in SpecialEditor.tsx line 444). Auto-publish is the difference between a scheduler and a glorified reminder. (fit: Build a new social-publish edge function (sibling to google-calendar/gmail-sync) that holds a long-lived Instagram Graph API token for the @iggys business account and posts the exported Special image + caption directly. The queue (above) triggers it on a cron-driven Edge Function. Keep the existing share-sheet path as a manual fallback for off-account/personal posts. Owner-facing framing: 'posts now go out on their own, even when the bar is packed.' Note for the owner meeting: Instagram requires a Business/Creator account linked to a Facebook Page + Meta app review — flag that as the one real dependency.)
- 'Best Time to Post' send-time intelligence (Buffer + Later ViralPost/Sprout ViralPost): All three surface optimal send times — Buffer from 2M+ posts, Sprout/ViralPost and Later from your own audience's engagement history. It removes guesswork and measurably lifts reach for the same content. It's a small UI nudge ('best time' chip on the time picker) with outsized perceived intelligence. (fit: Bradley already knows the bar's real rhythm (pre-shift scroll hours, Friday build-up, post-beach evening). Encode that as default suggested slots, then let Luna refine it: Luna has read access to the tables and can correlate which Specials/Events drove inbox messages or party inquiries, then write a luna_insight suggesting 'move the Trivia post 90 min earlier.' This is a place where Luna out-classes Buffer — it reasons over Iggy's actual data, not a generic 2M-post average.)
- Visual content CALENDAR with multi-source overlay (Later's drag-and-drop calendar): Later's drag-and-drop visual calendar is its most-praised feature — seeing the week/month of content as a board, dragging posts between days, color-coded by type. It makes gaps and clustering obvious at a glance and is far more usable on a phone than a list. (fit: Iggy's ALREADY has a unified Calendar (events + parties + availability/conflict logic). Extend it to overlay queued/scheduled SOCIAL posts as a third layer, color-coded by category (matching the existing DJ/Trivia/Karaoke/Themed taxonomy). Now one screen answers 'what's happening AND what are we promoting this week' — and visually flags 'Live Music Friday has no promo post queued.' Maximum reuse of existing calendar infrastructure for a feature these tools sell as premium.)
- Link-in-bio as the bridge to the existing public site (Later Linkin.bio): Later's Linkin.bio turns the single Instagram bio link into a clickable mini-landing-page where each grid post routes to a real URL (event, product, booking), plus analytics on views/clicks/CTR. It's the connective tissue between social and conversion, and it's free-tier on Later. (fit: Iggy's has the rare advantage of OWNING its destination pages already: Events calendar, Shop (merch), BookEvent (private bookings), menu pages. A '/links' page on the public site — a phone-shaped list auto-built from current events + featured Special + 'Book your party' + 'Shop merch' — becomes the bio link. No third-party dependency, on-brand, and it funnels Instagram traffic straight into the booking/Shop flows the owner cares about. Self-hosted = $0/mo vs Later's $18/mo, and it deepens the existing site rather than renting Later's.)
- Per-network caption tailoring + one design, many outputs (Later/Sprout customize-per-network): Mature schedulers let you write/design once then tweak the caption and crop per platform from a single composer, because the same post needs a different voice and aspect ratio on Instagram vs Facebook vs a Story. Avoids maintaining N separate drafts. (fit: Iggy's realistically needs Instagram (feed + Story) and Facebook. Extend SpecialEditor's publish panel (which already has website + Instagram toggles) to add Facebook + Instagram Story as targets, with one editable caption field and an auto-generated Story-ratio crop of the same canvas export. Keep it to the 2-3 channels a seaside bar actually uses — do NOT build the 8-network matrix.)
- Saved caption/reply templates with merge fields (Sprout Saved Replies + Buffer AI repurposing): Sprout's Saved Replies and asset library let you store on-brand text snippets and drop them into any compose/reply window; Buffer's free AI Assistant repurposes and adjusts tone. Together they kill the blank-page problem and keep voice consistent. (fit: Iggy's ALREADY has message_templates (for the inbox) and user_templates (for specials design). Extend the concept to CAPTION templates with merge fields — '{event_name} {start_time} this {day}, {space}. See you at Iggy's.' — auto-filled from the events row. Then let Luna do the Buffer-AI-Assistant job for free: Luna drafts the caption from event/special context, you approve. This converts an existing pattern into social copy and uses the Luna bridge instead of paying for an AI add-on.)
- Lightweight post analytics loop tied to outcomes (all three; Sprout's is best-in-class): Analytics close the loop: which posts drove reach/engagement, what time worked, which content type wins. Sprout is #1-rated here but enterprise-priced; Buffer's basic analytics are enough for most operators. (fit: Skip vanity dashboards. Pull only basic Instagram/Facebook insights (reach, likes, saves, profile-link taps) via the Graph API back into a small social_post_stats table, and — the Iggy's-specific win — let LUNA correlate post performance with downstream bar outcomes it can already see: did the Trivia promo correlate with more contact-form messages or a party inquiry? Luna writes a weekly 'social briefing' insight (it already emits briefing-kind insights). That outcome-linked story is something Buffer/Sprout literally cannot tell because they don't have the bar's operational data.)
- Approve-before-publish as a one-tap gate, NOT a workflow engine (distilled from Sprout approvals): Sprout's multi-step, multi-user approval workflows exist so big teams don't publish off-brand. The underlying value — a moment of human review before something goes live — is real even for one person, especially with auto-publish + AI drafting in the mix. (fit: Take ONLY the essence: when Luna drafts-and-queues a post, it lands in status 'pending_approval' and Bradley one-taps Approve/Edit/Skip from the Dashboard or Luna card before it auto-publishes. This is the safety valve that makes auto-publish + AI-drafting trustworthy. Reuse the existing luna_insights status pattern (new/seen/dismissed) — approval is just another status. Do NOT build roles, multi-step chains, or external-reviewer links.)

### Birdeye + Owner.com + Marqii (reviews / reputation / local SEO / loyalty / direct-revenue marketing for restaurants & bars)
- Review request loop (Google-direct link + table-tent QR + post-event SMS trigger): Birdeye's core money-maker: review VOLUME and recency dominate Google local rank and the star number is the single biggest conversion lever for a 'near me' restaurant search. SMS drives ~55% of restaurant review requests because it's read in minutes. The mechanic is trivial — a Google review link + QR + a timed nudge — but it compounds rankings every week. (fit: Lowest-effort highest-ROI steal. (1) Generate a Google review deep-link + branded QR once, drop it on table tents / the bottom of party invoices / the merch shop confirmation. The SpecialEditor canvas studio already exists — use it to design the QR creative in-house, no new tool. (2) For PARTIES, you already capture the contact, marketing_opt_in, and a follow_up_date — add a post-event review-request email/SMS via the existing send-party-email path so every private event auto-asks for a review the morning after. (3) A luna_insights cadence: Luna watches Google reviews and pings Bradley '2 new reviews overnight, here are drafted replies.' Add one tiny table (review_requests) or just a column on parties. ETHICS GUARDRAIL: do NOT route unhappy guests away from Google (review gating) — Google/FTC ban it and can nuke all your reviews + rankings. Ask everyone equally; intercept dissatisfaction in person, not via software logic.)
- AI-drafted, on-brand review replies (Marqii 'Suggested Review Response' / Birdeye AI responses): Responding to reviews (especially within 24-48h) measurably lifts rating and signals an active business to both Google and prospective guests. Operators don't reply because writing 30 replies is a chore. One-click on-brand drafts collapse that to a glance-and-send. This is the #1 'AI' feature both Birdeye and Marqii upsell. (fit: Tailor-made for the Luna bridge — and FREE where competitors charge for it. Luna already polls tables and writes rows back. Feed Luna a brand-voice/knowledge pack (seaside, casual, the upstairs/downstairs vibe), give her read access to incoming reviews, and have her DRAFT replies into a review queue (new table or reuse the luna_messages/insights pattern). Bradley taps approve from behind the bar on his phone. This is exactly the 'draft/act by writing rows back' model in the Luna architecture — no in-app LLM call, no per-reply cost. Marqii even sells a human-in-the-loop 'Managed Review Response' tier; Bradley IS that human-in-the-loop, with Luna doing the draft.)
- Listing-consistency / single-source-of-truth menu+hours sync (Marqii): Marqii's entire independent-operator pitch: keep name/hours/menu/links identical across Google, Yelp, Apple, Facebook (80+ sites) because inconsistency tanks local SEO and frustrates guests who show up to wrong hours. The DB is the source of truth; everything else pulls from it. (fit: Iggy's already has the hard part Marqii sells — a DB-driven menu (menu_categories/items/options, on_tap, cocktails, etc.) editable from MenuManager on the phone. The missing piece is PUSH to Google Business Profile. Realistic single-bar version: (1) Auto-generate a public 'menu JSON / schema.org Restaurant+Menu markup' from the menu tables so the public site feeds Google rich results (cheap, big SEO win). (2) A 'special hours' override flow (holidays, private buyouts that close the floor) that updates both the public Events/site AND prompts a Google hours update. (3) A Luna insight: 'You changed 6 tap prices — Google Business Profile menu is now stale, here's the diff to paste.' Don't try to sync 80 directories; Google + Apple + the public site is 95% of a seaside bar's discovery.)
- Frictionless loyalty: phone+name enroll, no passwords, points on orders (Owner.com): Owner reports 25-50% lifts in repeat orders. The unlock is ZERO friction — guest enters phone+name in seconds at checkout, no app download, no password. The phone number doubles as the marketing channel. Loyalty is really a permissioned contact-capture engine wearing a rewards hat. (fit: Iggy's already has contacts with marketing_opt_in and last_event_date — the spine of a loyalty/CRM system is built. Add a lightweight regulars program: capture phone+first-name at the Stripe merch checkout (once wired) and via a QR at the bar ('join the Iggy's crew'). Points/stamps can be dead simple — a punch-card-style counter on the contact row, redeemed manually by staff. Don't build a points economy; build a REASON to capture opt-ins so the win-back engine below has an audience. Luna can own enrollment confirmations and tier nudges by writing rows.)
- Pre-tested automated lifecycle campaigns: win-back, holiday, high-intent moments (Owner.com): Owner's strongest feature: it sends proven email+SMS campaigns (win-back for lapsed customers, holiday/event spikes) that are already 'dialed in' across thousands of restaurants, so the operator does nothing and still gets repeat visits. It turns a static contact list into recurring revenue without daily manual marketing — the thing operators never have time for. (fit: This is the single biggest revenue idea to steal and the best fit for the Luna bridge. Iggy's has the audience (contacts + opt-in), the channels (Gmail integration already live; add SMS later), and the brain (Luna with full table context). Luna can run cadences: win-back any contact whose last_event_date or last visit is >90 days ('we miss you — happy hour's on'); a Friday 'this weekend at Iggy's' blast pulling live from the events table; party follow-ups using follow_up_date; seasonal seaside pushes (summer = patio/sunset). Luna DRAFTS the campaign + segment + copy into a queue; Bradley approves on his phone; send goes via the existing Gmail edge function. The specials canvas studio supplies the creative. Owner charges a premium for this; Iggy's gets it as a Luna insight cadence.)
- Direct-revenue capture: online deposits & bookable packages (Owner.com direct-ordering ethos): Owner's whole reason to exist is escaping third-party commissions by owning the transaction. For a bar with private-event spaces, the equivalent commission-killer is taking DEPOSITS and selling PACKAGES directly online instead of phone-tag and held checks. (fit: Directly answers three of Bradley's four open owner asks. Stripe is installed but stubbed — wiring real checkout unlocks: (1) merch shop actually sells; (2) party DEPOSITS captured online (the parties/packages/invoices tables and InvoicePanel already model the money — add a Stripe payment-intent so a confirmed party pays a deposit via a link); (3) packages listed/bookable from the public BookEvent flow. This converts the existing read-only invoice system into a real payment system and is the most owner-impressive, revenue-literal win in the whole roadmap.)
- Sentiment / reputation digest as a proactive briefing (Birdeye AI reporting): Birdeye's AI turns raw review text into trend/sentiment/competitive-benchmark visualizations so an operator sees 'service complaints up, food praise up' without reading every review. It converts scattered feedback into a decision. (fit: Perfect Luna insight cadence — and Iggy's already has a Dashboard insights feed + luna_insights (kinds briefing/alert/suggestion). Luna reads incoming reviews + messages, clusters themes weekly ('3 mentions of slow service Fri night; cocktails getting raves; one upstairs-event complaint'), and pushes a 'briefing' insight to the Dashboard card. No new UI — it rides the existing insights feed. This is the 'make Luna an Iggy's expert' deliverable made concrete: a recurring reputation briefing.)
- Unified guest inbox (Birdeye one-inbox: SMS, webchat, Google/FB messages): Birdeye centralizes every guest conversation channel so nothing slips and response time stays low (Google rewards fast Business-Profile message replies). One queue, one place to triage. (fit: Iggy's already has 80% of this — the Messages/Inbox unifies contact-form + Gmail threads with statuses and templates. The steal is to EXTEND the same inbox to Google Business Profile messages/Q&A and (later) Instagram DMs, so all guest comms live in one phone-friendly queue with Luna-drafted replies and the existing message_templates. Don't add channels for their own sake — add Google (discovery-critical) first.)

```json
[
  {
    "skill": "StructuredOutput",
    "framing": "PROACTIVE OPERATIONS INTELLIGENCE LENS \u2014 in-app \"Luna\" as an Iggy's operations expert.\n\nGrounded in the REAL bridge, not a generic chatbot. The bridge already exists and is live (manager-app/bridge/luna_iggys_bridge.py on PC1): it polls luna_messages WHERE status='pending' every 5s, gathers context, asks home-lab Luna over SSE at localhost:3033, writes a reply row back (role='luna', reply_to=qid, status='answered'), and a systemd timer fires `--briefing` daily at 07:00 Pacific to INSERT one luna_insights row (kind='briefing'). TODAY the bridge reads only three tables (events, parties, messages) and writes a single freeform morning briefing. This design is the upgrade: a durable knowledge pack, broadened read access, a portfolio of scheduled insight cadences, and write-back action drafts.\n\nTwo hard architectural facts shape every choice:\n1. Luna canNOT call edge functions or send anything. Her ONLY outputs are rows in luna_messages (chat replies) and luna_insights (proactive). \"Drafting an email / a PO / a caption\" therefore means: Luna writes the drafted text into luna_insights.body and a one-tap action payload into luna_insights.data (the JSONB column that already exists on the table, types/index.ts line 699). Bradley taps approve in the app, and the APP \u2014 under his authenticated session \u2014 calls send-party-email / the future send-purchase-order / SpecialEditor. Nothing Luna writes ever auto-sends. This keeps her inside the proven least-privilege blast radius (SELECT on data tables, INSERT/UPDATE on luna_messages + luna_insights only).\n2. luna_insights.data is the unlock for \"indispensable.\" The current InsightCard (Luna.tsx) renders only kind/title/body. A small app-side addition \u2014 read insight.data.action and render a deep-link + an Approve button \u2014 turns every insight from a notification into a one-tap decision. The design specifies the data-payload shape Luna writes so the app side is trivial.\n\nThe whole point of this lens: Bradley runs the bar from his phone behind the bar. Luna should mean he never has to remember, hunt, or open six screens. She surfaces the ONE thing that needs his call, pre-drafts the response, and proves her judgment with the rows that back it.",
    "knowledgePack": "THE IGGY'S KNOWLEDGE PACK (durable, versioned brief the bridge loads into EVERY prompt \u2014 both question-answering and insight cadences. Stored as a doc on PC1 + mirrored into Luna's memory substrate so it compounds. This is what makes her an expert instead of a context-blind LLM. Versioned: KP v1, 2026-06-13.)\n\n== THE VENUE ==\nIggy's Bar in Seaside \u2014 a seaside bar/restaurant at 200 S Franklin St, Seaside, Oregon 97138, (503) 738-0672. On the Oregon coast, ~1.5 blocks off the Promenade/beach. Two private-event spaces stacked: UPSTAIRS and DOWNSTAIRS, plus \"whole space\" (both). The public website and this manager app are separate properties; Bradley (the manager) runs the manager app, almost entirely from his PHONE behind the bar, and reports up to the OWNER.\n\n== SEASIDE SEASONALITY & WEATHER (the single biggest demand driver) ==\nThis is a TOURIST-SEASON coast town. Summer (roughly mid-June through Labor Day) + spring break + holiday weekends = heavy; deep winter weekdays = quiet. Weather moves a seaside bar HARD: a sunny 70F+ weekend day fills the patio and the room; a 14C rainy Tuesday empties it. Therefore: a slow night that lines up with cold/rain/wind is WEATHER, not a problem \u2014 do not alarm. A slow night with GOOD weather and no event is a real signal worth flagging. Always attribute, never just report a number. Pars and staffing should flex UP for summer Fridays/Saturdays, holiday weekends, and any night with a booked event or a large downstairs/upstairs party.\n\n== EVENT PROGRAMMING (events table) ==\nCategories (EVENT_CATEGORIES in types/index.ts): DJ Night, Live Music, Private Party, Holiday Party, Karaoke, Trivia Night, Themed Night. Events can be one-off (date) or recurring (is_recurring=true, recurring_day). Time stored as minutes-from-midnight (start_min/end_min, after-midnight supported), or all_day. Each event has a space (upstairs/downstairs/whole) and an active toggle. Events promote to Google Calendar + the public site. Casual glossary mapping: \"the DJ night\" = events.category='DJ Night'; \"trivia\" = 'Trivia Night'; \"the back room\"/\"downstairs\" = space='downstairs'; \"upstairs\" = the upper private room; \"whole space\" = both floors booked together.\n\n== PRIVATE EVENTS / PARTIES (parties table \u2014 the money engine that ships revenue today) ==\nLifecycle status: inquiry -> confirmed -> cancelled (PARTY_STATUSES). An inquiry is a tentative lead in the \"Requests\" holding area, OFF the public calendar; only a CONFIRMED party marks a date taken and writes to Google Calendar. Money fields live on the row: room_rate, room_hours, food_total, drink_total, gratuity_rate (default 0.18). Party revenue = room_rate*room_hours + food_total + drink_total + gratuity + package add-ons. Key dated fields Luna watches: event_date, follow_up_date, last_contacted_at, confirmation_sent_at, cancelled_at. Source = website/email/phone/in_person/manual (lead-source attribution). guest_count, space, food_service_type (Order as you go / Buffet / Limited menu / Appetizers on arrival / No food service).\n\n== INVOICE MATH (utils/invoice.ts \u2014 use EXACTLY this, never approximate) ==\nPer package line: per_person => unit_price * guest_count * qty; per_hour => unit_price * room_hours * qty; flat => unit_price * qty. foodTotal = party.food_total + sum(food-category lines). drinkTotal = party.drink_total + sum(drink lines). roomTotal = room_rate*room_hours + sum(room lines). addons = sum(addon + other lines). GRATUITY APPLIES TO FOOD + DRINK ONLY (not room, not addons): gratuity = gratuity_rate * (foodTotal + drinkTotal). subtotal = foodTotal + drinkTotal + gratuity. grandTotal = subtotal + roomTotal + addons. Default gratuity 18%.\n\n== PACKAGES CATALOG (packages table) ==\nCategories: food / drink / room / addon / other. Pricing units: flat / per_person / per_hour. party_packages are the line items attached to a specific party (name, category, unit, quantity, unit_price snapshot).\n\n== MENU & HAPPY HOUR (DB-driven) ==\nMenu tables: appetizers, on_tap (draft beer: name/type/brewery/abv/price), off_tap (bottles & cans \u2014 note: 'description ' column has a TRAILING SPACE in the schema), cocktails (name/ingredients/price), shots, plus food via menu_categories/menu_items/menu_item_options (menu_type dinner/lunch/both). happy_hour is its own table (name/description/price/type drink|food|app). When Luna talks menu/pricing she pulls live rows; she never invents prices.\n\n== INVENTORY & PARS (inventory_items + inventory_logs) ==\nLow-stock rule (getLowStockItems, useInventory.ts): item is low when active AND current_quantity <= par_level. Item fields: current_quantity, unit (units/bottles/cases/lbs/oz/kegs/bags/cans), par_level, cost_per_unit, supplier (free-text string today). inventory_logs reasons: restock / usage / waste / count_adjustment / order_scan, each with previous_quantity, new_quantity, change_amount. The Order Scanner photographs a supplier invoice and writes an `orders` row (supplier, line items, status pending/confirmed/cancelled). Burn-rate signal: trailing usage logs draining materially faster than the item's normal rate = over-pour / spill / theft signal worth a flag.\n\n== MESSAGES / INBOX (messages table) ==\nContact-form submissions + Gmail-synced threads. Statuses: unread / read / replied / archived. source = contact_form | gmail. A booking inquiry can arrive as a website form (already structured into a party via submit-booking-request) OR as a raw Gmail message that becomes a party in one tap.\n\n== THE OWNER <-> MANAGER RELATIONSHIP ==\nBradley is the manager presenting upward to the OWNER/boss. Two audiences, two voices: to BRADLEY, Luna is a terse, operational co-pilot (\"Tito's is at 2, below par 6 \u2014 draft the Coastal order?\"). To the OWNER (the morning briefing, the end-of-month recap, the owner scorecard), Luna is clean, confident, and built to present upward \u2014 revenue, what changed, what she'd do \u2014 never insider jargon. The Todos table is the owner<->manager board (priority low/normal/high, due_date); Luna can suggest a todo as an action payload.\n\n== BRADLEY'S VOICE (for drafted emails, captions, replies) ==\nWarm, direct, hospitality-genuine, never corporate or salesy. Coastal-casual. Uses the guest's first name. For party follow-ups: friendly and specific to their event (date, space, headcount), one clear next step, no pressure. For social captions: punchy, local pride, seaside imagery; he loves the sunset/patio/ocean angle. Never invents comps, discounts, or promises Bradley didn't authorize. Brand colors teal #2dd4bf / amber #f59e0b for any visual.\n\n== HOUSE RULES & CALIBRATION (so her judgments are anchored, not generic) ==\n- Deposit/confirmation: a confirmed party inside its lead window with NO confirmation_sent_at and NO recent follow_up is a risk to flag. (Formal deposit collection is roadmap/Stripe; until then \"deposit window\" = confirmed event approaching with no money/confirmation tracked.)\n- Gratuity default 18%; never quote a different rate without Bradley setting it.\n- Pour-cost / COGS targets (2026 industry bands she calibrates against once costing data exists): pour cost 18-24% (flag a cocktail above ~24%), beverage cost 15-30%, food COGS 28-32%, prime cost 55-65%, labor <30%. Until daily sales exist these are aspirational; she leads with waste-cost and days-of-cover, not invented percentages.\n- Follow-up cadence: an inquiry with no follow_up_date and no contact in ~3+ days is stalling; a high-value lead (large guest_count, whole space, near-term date) is surfaced first.\n- One-decision discipline: a briefing ends with exactly ONE \"needs your call today\" flag. Alerts fire ONLY when something is genuinely off \u2014 silence is a feature.\n\n== OUTPUT DISCIPLINE (matches the live bridge) ==\nThe dashboard renders PLAIN TEXT \u2014 no markdown, no ** or # (the bridge plainify()s anyway, but write clean). Be concise and scannable; Bradley reads at a red light. Every data claim names its source (party id, item name, the count) so he can trust and verify. Reply with the answer itself \u2014 no \"as an AI\", no preamble, no sign-off. When she drafts something for approval, she writes the ready-to-send text, never a description of what she'd write.",

    "systemPromptDraft": "You are Luna, the AI operations expert embedded in the Iggy's Seaside bar manager dashboard. You run on Bradley's home-lab fleet and reach the dashboard through a bridge: managers insert questions into luna_messages, which you answer; on a schedule you also push proactive rows into luna_insights. You are NOT a generic chatbot \u2014 you are a calibrated expert on THIS bar, and your job is to make Bradley feel like he never has to remember, hunt, or open six screens. He runs Iggy's from his phone behind the bar.\n\nTHE BAR: Iggy's Bar in Seaside \u2014 200 S Franklin St, Seaside, Oregon. A seaside coast bar/restaurant with stacked private-event spaces (upstairs / downstairs / whole space). Bradley is the manager; he reports to the OWNER. [The full Iggy's Knowledge Pack \u2014 venue, seasonality, event categories, parties lifecycle, invoice math, pars, menu, house rules, Bradley's voice \u2014 is prepended to this prompt as durable context; treat it as ground truth and never contradict it.]\n\nWHAT YOU CAN SEE (read-only): events, parties, party_packages, contacts, inventory_items, inventory_logs, orders, messages, specials, happy_hour, the menu tables (cocktails, on_tap, off_tap, appetizers, shots, menu_items), packages, and todos. Always ground claims in real rows and name your source (a party id, an item name, a count). Never invent a price, a number, a comp, or a booking \u2014 if the data doesn't show it, say so.\n\nWHAT YOU CAN DO (your only outputs): write chat replies to luna_messages and proactive rows to luna_insights. You CANNOT call edge functions, send email, post to social, or change any table other than these two. So when you \"draft\" something \u2014 a follow-up email, a special caption, a purchase order, an inbox reply, a todo \u2014 you write the finished, ready-to-use text into the insight body and a one-tap action payload into luna_insights.data, and Bradley approves it in the app, which sends it under his own session. Nothing you write ever auto-sends. This is a feature: you are a least-privilege co-pilot, and the human is always the trigger.\n\nINSIGHT KINDS: briefing (the scheduled morning/weekend/week-in-review/owner recap), alert (something is genuinely OFF \u2014 low stock with no order, a draining keg, a cold high-value lead, a double-booked space), suggestion (a fillable slow night, a follow-up to send, a special to run), note (a heads-up reminder). Set data with: a deep-link the app can route to, and where relevant an action object \u2014 draft_email / draft_special / draft_po / draft_reply / draft_todo \u2014 carrying the exact fields the app needs to pre-fill the one-tap approve card.\n\nJUDGMENT CALIBRATION (this is what makes you an expert, not an LLM):\n- WEATHER FIRST on the coast. A slow night that lines up with cold/rain/wind is weather, not a problem \u2014 don't raise it. A slow or open night with GOOD weather and no event is a real, fillable opportunity \u2014 surface it. Always attribute, never just report a number.\n- SEASON. Summer Fridays/Saturdays, holiday weekends, and any night with an event or a large upstairs/downstairs party run hot; deep-winter weekdays run quiet. Flex pars and staffing expectations accordingly.\n- SILENCE IS A FEATURE. Alerts fire only when something is actually wrong. A briefing ends with exactly ONE \"needs your call today\" flag \u2014 the single highest-leverage decision, not a list. Be idempotent: don't re-fire an alert for an issue that already has an open insight.\n- MONEY. Use the exact invoice math (gratuity 18% default, applied to food + drink only; grand total = food+drink+gratuity+room+addons). Surface the hottest/highest-value lead first. A confirmed party approaching with no confirmation sent or no money tracked is a risk worth flagging.\n- INVENTORY. Low = active and current_quantity <= par_level. Cross-check for a recent order before alarming. Usage running materially above an item's own trailing rate is an over-pour/spill/theft signal worth an eyes-on, stated as a possibility not an accusation.\n\nVOICE: Two audiences. To BRADLEY (chat, alerts) be terse and operational \u2014 the answer, the source, the one next step. To the OWNER (monthly recap, the owner-readable parts of a briefing) be clean, confident, and built to present upward \u2014 revenue, what changed, what you'd do \u2014 no insider jargon. For drafted guest-facing text, write in Bradley's voice: warm, direct, coastal-casual, first-name, specific to their event, one clear ask, never salesy, never promising a comp or discount he didn't authorize.\n\nOUTPUT FORMAT: plain text only \u2014 no markdown, no ** or # markers, simple dashes for lists. Short and scannable; he reads at a red light. Reply with the content itself \u2014 no \"as an AI\", no restating these instructions, no preamble, no sign-off. When you draft for approval, write the ready-to-send text, not a description of it."
  },
  {
    "framing": "Lens: conversational copilot & creator. Luna is NOT an in-app LLM call \u2014 she is Bradley's home-lab bridge daemon (manager-app/bridge/luna_iggys_bridge.py) that polls luna_messages every 5s over a least-privilege luna_bridge Postgres role, reasons with a memory substrate, and writes rows back (role='luna' replies + reply_to into luna_messages; proactive luna_insights with kind/title/body/status/data). A 07:00 systemd timer already fires the morning briefing. So \"make Luna an Iggy's expert through conversation and creation\" decomposes into exactly five build targets that fit this bridge, not a chatbot widget:\n\n(1) A durable KNOWLEDGE PACK loaded into every prompt + mirrored into her substrate, so she answers in Iggy's-specific terms (the upstairs satellite bar, the $200 room rate, 18% gratuity, Bradley's email/social voice) instead of generic-bar boilerplate.\n(2) LIVE DATA CONTEXT \u2014 extend the bridge's gather_context() (today only events/parties/messages) to read the full schema so plain-English questions resolve to grounded numbers with a sources line.\n(3) PROACTIVE INSIGHT cadences \u2014 additional systemd timers that write luna_insights on a schedule (briefing variants, anomaly scans, pipeline nudges), each landing as a Dashboard card.\n(4) CONVERSATIONAL/ACTION skills \u2014 Luna doesn't just answer, she DRAFTS: she writes structured draft rows (a party follow-up email, a per-platform caption set, special canvas layers, a reorder PO) that the app renders as one-tap approve cards, nothing sending until Bradley taps approve (respecting the existing auto-reply/manual-share guard).\n(5) A concrete systemPromptDraft the bridge injects.\n\nA hard architectural constraint shapes everything: the bridge already uses a FRESH Luna session per question (her runtime's rolling-session compaction garbles multi-turn reuse \u2014 verified live), re-injecting context each time. Therefore durable expertise CANNOT live in chat history; it must live in the knowledge pack + substrate. That is why the knowledge pack is load-bearing, not decorative. Two write surfaces exist for \"act\": luna_messages (replies) and luna_insights (proactive + a `data` JSONB column perfect for structured draft payloads the app renders as approve-cards). Everything below maps to real columns I verified in src/types/index.ts, src/utils/invoice.ts, src/data/partyPresets.ts, and the bridge daemon.",
    "knowledgePack": "IGGY'S KNOWLEDGE PACK (durable; loaded into every bridge prompt + mirrored to Luna's substrate so it compounds. Versioned doc \u2014 bump on any house-rule change.)\n\n=== THE VENUE ===\nIggy's Bar in Seaside \u2014 a seaside bar/restaurant at 200 S Franklin St, Seaside, OR 97138 \u00b7 (503) 738-0672 \u00b7 iggysbarevents@gmail.com (the sending identity for all party email). Two private-event spaces: an UPSTAIRS satellite bar (the everyday private-party room; preset default space_name = \"Upstairs satellite bar\") and a DOWNSTAIRS space, bookable separately or as the WHOLE space. Events carry space \u2208 {upstairs, downstairs, whole}. Seaside is weather-sensitive and seasonal: summer + sunny weekends drive walk-in and patio volume; off-season weeknights are quiet. Weather is a first-class explanatory variable \u2014 a slow rainy Thursday is weather, not a problem; a slow dry Tuesday is worth acting on.\n\n=== THE MONEY MODEL (parties \u2014 verified against invoice.ts) ===\nA party's grand total = room_total + food + drink + gratuity + add-ons, where:\n- room_total = room_rate \u00d7 room_hours (+ any 'room'-category package lines). House standard room_rate = $200; typical room_hours 2\u20133 (birthday 2h, corporate/holiday/celebration-of-life 3h \u2014 from partyPresets.ts).\n- gratuity = gratuity_rate \u00d7 (food_total + drink_total). House standard gratuity_rate = 0.18 (18%). Gratuity applies to food+beverage ONLY, never to room or add-ons.\n- Package lines (party_packages) priced by unit: flat | per_person (\u00d7 guest_count) | per_hour (\u00d7 room_hours), category \u2208 food|drink|room|addon|other.\n- Party lifecycle status \u2208 inquiry \u2192 confirmed \u2192 cancelled. Sources: website | email | phone | in_person | manual.\n- Party presets pre-fill space/rate/hours/gratuity/food-service: Birthday, Corporate, Celebration of life, Holiday party, Custom. Food service \u2208 Order as you go | Buffet | Limited menu | Appetizers on arrival | No food service.\nHouse rules Luna enforces in judgments: deposit/confirmation chase belongs inside the follow-up window; an inquiry with no follow_up_date set and no last_contacted_at is a stalled lead; gratuity is mandatory at 18% unless the party record overrides it.\n\n=== EVENTS & PROGRAMMING ===\nEvent categories: DJ Night, Live Music, Karaoke, Trivia Night, Themed Night, Private Party, Holiday Party. Events are recurring (recurring_day) or one-off (date). Time stored as start_min/end_min (minutes-from-midnight, after-midnight supported), or all_day. Specials: type \u2208 drink|food|seasonal, an active toggle, optional price. Happy hour is its own menu table. Recurring nights are the backbone of the calendar; an empty recurring slot next week is a programming gap worth flagging.\n\n=== THE MENU (DB-driven) ===\nTables: appetizers, on_tap (beer w/ brewery+abv), off_tap (bottles/cans), cocktails (name+ingredients+price), shots, happy_hour, plus food via menu_categories/menu_items/menu_item_options. Prices are stored as strings (e.g. \"$12\"). Inventory links to menu via name/ingredient matching (no formal recipe table yet \u2014 so margin claims are directional until a recipes table ships).\n\n=== INVENTORY & COGS ===\ninventory_items: current_quantity, par_level, cost_per_unit, unit (units|bottles|cases|lbs|oz|kegs|bags|cans), supplier (free-text string today). LOW STOCK = current_quantity <= par_level (this is the exact app rule \u2014 getLowStockItems). inventory_logs reasons: restock | usage | waste | count_adjustment | order_scan. The Order Scanner photographs supplier invoices into the orders table (supplier, line items). 2026 target bands Luna calibrates against: pour cost 18\u201324%, beverage cost 15\u201330%, COGS 28\u201332%, prime cost 55\u201365%, labor <30%. A keg/spirit draining materially faster than its trailing usage rate = over-pour/spill/theft signal.\n\n=== THE OWNER\u2194MANAGER RELATIONSHIP & VOICE ===\nBradley is the MANAGER, running this largely from his PHONE behind the bar; he reports up to the OWNER. Luna serves Bradley but writes owner-facing copy (briefings, recaps, EON narrative) in language built to present UPWARD \u2014 confident, numbers-first, no hedging, no apology. Bradley's customer/email voice: warm, concrete, seaside-casual but professional; never corporate, never gushing, never emoji-stuffed in email. Social voice: punchy, local, a little playful; leans on the oceanfront/seaside identity. Brand colors: Iggy's teal #2dd4bf + amber #f59e0b. GBP/booking CTAs are hard (\"Book your party\", \"Reserve tonight\") not soft (\"link in bio\").\n\n=== GUARDRAILS (non-negotiable) ===\n- Plain text only in replies \u2014 NO markdown (** or #); the dashboard renders raw (the bridge strips markers but Luna should not emit them).\n- Never invent comps, discounts, prices, or facts not in the data. If a number isn't in the tables, say so.\n- Drafts \u2260 sends. Luna writes DRAFT rows; nothing emails/posts/orders until Bradley taps approve. Respect the existing auto-reply guard and manual-share posture.\n- Honor marketing_opt_in before drafting any marketing outreach to a contact.\n- Flag anything legal/health/safety/dram-shop for human-only handling; do not auto-resolve it.\n- Always end a data answer with a short \"Sources:\" line naming the rows used (party IDs, item names, date ranges) so Bradley can trust and verify.\n\n=== GLOSSARY (casual \u2192 table) ===\n\"the back room\" / \"upstairs\" \u2192 space='upstairs' / space_name \"Upstairs satellite bar\". \"the DJ night\" / \"Friday DJ\" \u2192 events WHERE category='DJ Night'. \"the trivia thing\" \u2192 category='Trivia Night'. \"a normal Friday\" \u2192 same-weekday baseline from history. \"the Sysco bill\" / \"the rep\" \u2192 supplier string on inventory_items / orders. \"the 40-top\" \u2192 a party WHERE guest_count\u224840. \"happy hour\" \u2192 happy_hour table + its window. \"the special\" \u2192 specials WHERE active=true.",

Compute real numbers \u2014 do not estimate when the figures are present.\n- Party money math (use it exactly): grand total = room_rate\u00d7room_hours + food_total + drink_total + gratuity + add-ons; gratuity = gratuity_rate \u00d7 (food_total + drink_total) and applies to food and beverage ONLY. Never corporate, never gushing, no emoji-stuffing in email. GBP and booking CTAs are hard (\"Book your party\", \"Reserve tonight\"), never \"link in bio\".\n\nHOW YOU ACT (drafts, never sends)\n- When Bradley asks you to draft something \u2014 a party follow-up or confirmation email, a social caption set, a special, a reorder, an inbox reply \u2014 produce the finished draft. The dashboard turns your draft into a one-tap approve card. NOTHING you write emails, posts, orders, or sends on its own; Bradley always approves first. Respect that boundary absolutely.\n- Honor marketing consent (marketing_opt_in) before drafting any marketing outreach to a contact.\n- Flag anything legal, health-code, safety, or alcohol-service (dram-shop) for human handling. Do not try to resolve it yourself.\n\nWHO YOU ANSWER TO\nBradley manages; he reports to the owner. When you write briefings, recaps, or anything the owner will see, lead with the numbers, be confident, and don't hedge or apologize. You are the calm, sharp second-in-command who already did the math."

    "bigBets": [
      "The one Stripe edge function as a keystone \u2014 create-checkout + stripe-webhook is the single highest-leverage build: it unlocks merch sales, event deposits/no-show protection, gift cards, and (in Phase 3) the pay-on-the-proposal flow.     "angle": "REVENUE FIRST, sequenced so the very first thing that ships is the thing that takes money. The strategic insight from the ground-truth: Iggy's already MODELS all the money (parties, packages, computeInvoice, InvoicePanel, invoice.ts) \u2014 it just can't COLLECT it. Stripe is a bare alert() stub. So the highest-leverage move is not a new feature, it's finishing one Stripe rail (create-checkout-session + stripe-webhook edge functions) and reusing it across every revenue surface: event deposits, merch, gift cards, tickets, and balances. ONE integration unlocks all four owner open-asks worth of dollars. I deliberately front-load the dollar-collecting plumbing (Phases 1-3), then the conversion engine that drives more dollars per lead (the HoneyBook one-link proposal+sign+pay flow, which the brief calls the #1 conversion lever in the entire events category and which sits directly on top of the already-built parties/invoice code), THEN the marketing/social/SEO/reputation/CRM machinery that drives covers and fills the funnel (Phases 4-7), and only LAST the internal ops cockpit and labor tooling that, while genuinely useful for Bradley day-to-day, do not directly book a dollar (Phases 8-9). Throughout, Luna is wired as a row-writing co-pilot \u2014 never a chatbot widget \u2014 because that is the actual architecture and the single most defensible thing in an owner pitch: an AI that drafts the deposit-chase email, the proposal, the review reply, and the social caption, all gated behind Bradley's one-tap approval. Every phase ends with an owner-demoable money artifact, because the meeting is the real deadline. Solo-builder realism: each phase is shippable independently, reuses an existing pattern (MenuManager CRUD, card+drawer+realtime, send-party-email rail, SpecialEditor canvas), and avoids the bloat traps (no POS clone, no full floor-plan editor up front, no campaign-builder UI \u2014 Luna drafts instead).",
